```
Here is a comprehensive report of everything found about what makes a SKILL.md compliant with the agentskills.io specification.

---

## Complete agentskills.io SKILL.md Specification

### Directory Structure

A skill is a directory containing, at minimum, a `SKILL.md` file:

```
skill-name/
├── SKILL.md          # Required: metadata + instructions
├── scripts/          # Optional: executable code
├── references/       # Optional: documentation
├── assets/           # Optional: templates, resources
└── ...               # Any additional files
```

### SKILL.md File Format

The file must contain **YAML frontmatter** (delimited by `---`) followed by **Markdown body content**.

---

### YAML Frontmatter Fields

#### Required Fields

| Field | Max Length | Constraints |
|-------|-----------|-------------|
| `name` | 64 chars | Lowercase letters, numbers, hyphens only. Must not start/end with hyphen. No consecutive hyphens (`--`). Must match the parent directory name exactly (after Unicode NFKC normalization). |
| `description` | 1024 chars | Non-empty. Should describe what the skill does AND when to use it. Should include specific keywords for agent discovery. |

#### Optional Fields

| Field | Max Length | Notes |
|-------|-----------|-------|
| `license` | -- | License name or reference to a bundled license file. |
| `compatibility` | 500 chars | Environment requirements (intended product, system packages, network access). |
| `metadata` | -- | Arbitrary string-to-string key-value mapping for additional properties. |
| `allowed-tools` | -- | Space-delimited list of pre-approved tools (experimental). |

**Only these 6 fields are allowed.** Any extra/unknown fields trigger a validation error.

---

### What `skills-ref validate` Checks

The `skills-ref validate ./my-skill` command performs two categories of checks:

#### 1. Metadata Validation (no I/O, works on parsed data)

- **`name` field:**
  - Must be a non-empty string
  - Must not exceed 64 characters
  - Must be entirely lowercase
  - Cannot start or end with a hyphen
  - Cannot contain consecutive hyphens (`--`)
  - Only alphanumeric characters (`a-z`, `0-9`) and hyphens (`-`) permitted
  - Unicode normalization (NFKC) is applied for consistent comparison

- **`description` field:**
  - Must be a non-empty string
  - Cannot exceed 1024 characters

- **`compatibility` field (if present):**
  - Must be a string
  - Cannot exceed 500 characters

- **No unknown frontmatter fields allowed** -- only `name`, `description`, `license`, `allowed-tools`, `metadata`, `compatibility`

#### 2. Directory Validation (with file I/O)

- The directory must exist and be a valid directory
- Must contain a `SKILL.md` file (case-insensitive: accepts `SKILL.md` preferred, `skill.md` as fallback)
- The skill `name` must match the parent directory name exactly

#### 3. Parsing Validation

- Frontmatter must start with `---` delimiter
- Frontmatter must be properly closed with a second `---`
- YAML must be a valid mapping (uses `strictyaml` for strict parsing)
- `metadata` field, if present, is converted to string-to-string mapping

---

### Minimal Compliant Example

```markdown
---
name: skill-name
description: A description of what this skill does and when to use it.
---
```

### Full Example with Optional Fields

```markdown
---
name: pdf-processing
description: Extract PDF text, fill forms, merge files. Use when handling PDFs.
license: Apache-2.0
compatibility: Requires python3, poppler-utils
metadata:
  author: example-org
  version: "1.0"
allowed-tools: Bash(python3:*) Read
---

## Instructions

Step-by-step instructions for the agent go here...
```

---

### Body Content Best Practices

The Markdown body has **no format restrictions** per the spec, but recommended practices include:

- Step-by-step instructions
- Examples of inputs and outputs
- Common edge cases
- Keep SKILL.md under **500 lines** / **< 5000 tokens**
- Use progressive disclosure: metadata loads at startup (~100 tokens), full body loads on activation, resource files load on-demand
- Move detailed reference material to separate files in `scripts/`, `references/`, `assets/`
- Keep file references one level deep from SKILL.md

### Additional Best Practices from Anthropic

From the memory database (observation #466, sourced from Anthropic's best practices guide):

- Frontmatter `description` should include **negative triggers** (what the skill is NOT for) to prevent false activation
- Instructions should use **third-person imperative voice** and step-by-step numbering
- Scripts should include **descriptive error messages** enabling agent self-correction
- Use **identical terminology** consistently throughout
- Place concrete templates in `assets/` rather than inline prose descriptions
- Never create human-oriented docs (README, CHANGELOG) inside the skill directory

---

### Known Issue in This Project

From memory observation #719 (today): The quick-mode skill generation workflow in bmad-module-skill-forge was found to produce output that **lacks a compliant SKILL.md file**, causing `npx skills add` to fail with "No valid skills found. Skills require a SKILL.md with name and description." The `skills-ref validate` tool is not yet integrated into the generation pipeline.

Perfect! Now I have enough information. Let me compile my findings.

## Summary: Test-Skill Workflow Validation & YAML Frontmatter Analysis

Based on my thorough reading of the test-skill and quick-skill workflow step files and supporting documentation, here's what I found:

---

### 1. **Test-Skill Workflow Validation Architecture**

The test-skill workflow performs **two-mode completeness verification** with validation at multiple levels:

**Step-by-step validation flow:**

1. **Step 01 (Init)** - `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md`
   - Validates that `SKILL.md` exists (hard requirement — workflow halts if missing)
   - Validates `metadata.json` exists (warning only if missing)
   - Loads forge tier state from `forge-tier.yaml`
   - Creates output test report from template

2. **Step 02 (Detect Mode)** - `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-02-detect-mode.md`
   - Determines **naive mode** (individual skills) vs **contextual mode** (stack skills)
   - Mode determines validation depth: coverage checks are same, but coherence checks differ

3. **Step 03 (Coverage Check)** - `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-03-coverage-check.md`
   - Compares documented exports in SKILL.md against actual source API surface
   - Uses subprocess optimization (Pattern 2) for AST analysis when forge tier allows
   - Validates per-export: is it documented? Does signature match? Are types covered?
   - Depth scales: Quick tier (name matching only), Forge tier (ast-grep signatures), Deep tier (gh CLI repo verification)

4. **Step 04 (Coherence Check)** - `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md`
   - **Naive mode**: Basic structural validation only (missing sections, broken markdown, self-contradictions)
   - **Contextual mode**: Full reference validation (file path refs, skill refs, type imports, integration patterns)
   - Validates that every cross-reference in SKILL.md points to real files/skills/types
   - For each reference, verifies target exists and signature matches

5. **Step 05 (Score)** - `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-05-score.md`
   - Calculates completeness score from coverage + coherence results
   - Uses weighting rules from `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/data/scoring-rules.md`
   - In naive mode, coherence weight redistributes to coverage

6. **Step 06 (Report)** - `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-06-report.md`
   - Generates final test report with score, gap analysis, and remediation guidance

---

### 2. **YAML Frontmatter Validation — Current State**

**Critical Finding: SKILL.md files currently lack YAML frontmatter in the generated output.**

The `agentskills-spec.md` specifies that SKILL.md **must** have frontmatter:

```yaml
---
name: library-name
description: >
  What the skill does and when to use it. Include specific keywords
  for agent discovery. Mention what NOT to use it for.
---
```

**However:**

1. **Quick-Skill workflow** (`/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/`):
   - Step 04 (compile) assembles SKILL.md from `skill-template.md`
   - The template (`/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md`) does NOT include frontmatter
   - Step 05 (validate) checks for frontmatter with `validate_frontmatter()` tool, but only if the tool is available
   - **If the tool is unavailable, validation is skipped with a warning** (line 59-60 in step-05-validate.md)
   - Step 06 writes SKILL.md directly without adding frontmatter

2. **Create-Skill workflow** (`/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/`):
   - Step 06 validates via `skills_ref.validate_frontmatter()` (auto-fixes if skills_ref available)
   - Documentation shows frontmatter should be: `name`, `version`, `description`, `author`
   - But the actual SKILL.md output structure suggests this validation is tool-dependent

3. **Test-Skill workflow**:
   - Step 01 does NOT check for frontmatter in SKILL.md
   - Step 03 (coverage check) extracts documented exports but doesn't validate frontmatter
   - No explicit frontmatter validation step

---

### 3. **References to "skills-ref", "agentskills.io", "specification", etc.**

**Key files with validation references:**

- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/agentskills-spec.md` - **The official specification** defining YAML frontmatter requirements (lines 28-40)
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-06-validate.md` - Uses `skills_ref.validate_frontmatter()` and `skills_ref.validate_schema()`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-05-validate.md` - Advisory validation (no `skills_ref` tool used, validation is checklist-based)
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-08-validate.md` - Validates output structure but not frontmatter specifically

---

### 4. **Template Files & SKILL.md Generation**

**Templates found:**

1. **Quick-Skill template**: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md`
   - Defines required sections: Overview, Description, Key Exports, Usage Patterns
   - Defines optional sections: Configuration, Dependencies, Notes
   - **Does NOT include frontmatter in the template structure**
   - No frontmatter is generated during step-04 (compile)

2. **Create-Skill template**: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md`
   - Referenced in step-05-compile.md
   - Specifies frontmatter structure (name, version, description, author)
   - **This suggests frontmatter IS supposed to be included**

3. **Stack-Skill template**: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/data/stack-skill-template.md`
   - Also defines frontmatter requirements

---

### **CONCLUSION: The Gap**

**What's missing/inconsistent:**

1. **Quick-Skill generates SKILL.md without YAML frontmatter**, despite the agentskills.io specification requiring it
2. **Quick-Skill validation (step-05) only checks for frontmatter if `skills_ref` tool is available** — otherwise it skips this check
3. **Test-Skill workflow does NOT validate for frontmatter presence** — it only validates coverage and coherence of the documented content
4. **No validation failures block the workflow** — validation failures are advisory only (in quick-skill) or handled gracefully (in create-skill)

**This means:**
- A quick-skill-generated SKILL.md lacking frontmatter would pass test-skill without warning
- The agentskills.io spec requirement for frontmatter is not consistently enforced across workflows
- Test-skill validates "what's documented" but doesn't validate "format compliance"

**Recommended investigation points:**
- Check `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md` line 1-10 to see if frontmatter is supposed to be there
- Review the `skills_ref` tool documentation (external to this repo) to understand when validation runs
- Check actual generated SKILL.md files in output folders to see if they include frontmatter in practice

> AGENT

Great research complete. Here's the full picture, Armel:

## Analysis: Where Compliance Fails

### The Root Causes (3 gaps found)

**Gap 1 — Quick-Skill Template Missing Frontmatter**
`src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md` does not include YAML frontmatter. The SKILL.md is born non-compliant.

**Gap 2 — Quick-Skill Validate Step is Advisory-Only**
`src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-05-validate.md` only checks frontmatter if `skills_ref` tool is available — otherwise silently skips it.

**Gap 3 — Test-Skill Never Validates Format Compliance**
The test-skill workflow validates *content completeness* (exports coverage, coherence) but never validates *format compliance* (frontmatter presence, allowed fields, name matching directory).

### What `skills-ref validate` Actually Checks

Per the agentskills.io spec, only **6 frontmatter fields** are allowed:

| Field | Required | Constraint |
|-------|----------|------------|
| `name` | **yes** | Lowercase, a-z/0-9/hyphens, must match directory name |
| `description` | **yes** | Non-empty, max 1024 chars |
| `license` | no | License name or file reference |
| `compatibility` | no | Max 500 chars |
| `metadata` | no | String-to-string key-value map |
| `allowed-tools` | no | Space-delimited tool list |

**Unknown fields cause validation failure.** So the earlier fix that added `name` and `description` was correct, but the module needs to prevent this from happening in the first place.

---

## Where Should Compliance Live?

My recommendation — **two places, layered**:

1. **Generation time** (quick-skill step-04 compile + template) — Make the template include frontmatter so SKILL.md is born compliant. This is the primary fix.

2. **Test time** (test-skill step-01 init) — Add a structural pre-check for frontmatter presence and required fields. This catches skills from any workflow, not just quick-skill.

The export-skill workflow is too late — by then users have already reviewed and approved the skill.

---

**What would you like to edit?**

- **[W]orkflows** — Fix the quick-skill template + validate step, and add frontmatter check to test-skill
- **[A]ll above** — I'll walk through each fix sequentially

Which approach, or do you want to scope this differently?

   109→  "confidence": "{extraction confidence}"
   110→}
   111→```
   112→
   113→### 5. Present Compiled Output for Review
   114→
   115→"**Compilation complete. Review before validation:**
   116→
   117→---
   118→
   119→**SKILL.md Preview:**
   120→
   121→{Display the full assembled SKILL.md content}
   122→
   123→---
   124→
   125→**context-snippet.md:**
   126→
   127→{Display the two-line snippet}
   128→
   129→---
   130→
   131→**metadata.json:**
   132→
   133→{Display the JSON}
   134→
   135→---
   136→
   137→**Extraction confidence:** {confidence}
   138→**Exports documented:** {count}
   139→
   140→Review the output above. When ready, continue to validation."
   141→
   142→### 6. Present MENU OPTIONS
   143→
   144→Display: **Select:** [C] Continue to Validation
   145→
   146→#### Menu Handling Logic:
   147→
   148→- IF C: Load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
   149→- IF Any other: Help user adjust compiled output, then redisplay menu
   150→
   151→#### EXECUTION RULES:
   152→
   153→- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting compiled output
   154→- ONLY proceed to validation when user selects 'C'
   155→- User can request changes to the compiled output before proceeding
   156→
   157→## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
   158→
   159→ONLY WHEN the user reviews the compiled output and selects 'C' will you load and read fully `{nextStepFile}` to execute validation.
   160→
   161→---
   162→
   163→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   164→
   165→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   166→
   167→- SKILL.md assembled with all available sections populated
   168→- context-snippet.md generated in correct two-line ADR-L format
   169→- metadata.json generated with source_authority: community
   170→- Compiled output presented for user review
   171→- User confirms readiness before proceeding to validation
   172→
   173→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   174→
   175→- Writing files to disk (that's step-06)
   176→- Skipping the review checkpoint
   177→- Not following template structure from {skillTemplateData}
   178→- Hallucinating exports or data not in extraction_inventory
   179→
   180→**Master Rule:** Skipping steps, optimizing sequences, or not following exact instructions is FORBIDDEN and constitutes SYSTEM FAILURE.
   181→

```
     1→# agentskills.io Specification
     2→
     3→## Principle
     4→
     5→Skills produced by Skill Forge comply with the agentskills.io specification — a format designed for agent consumption through progressive disclosure. The format prioritizes discoverability at low token cost, deterministic procedural instructions, and just-in-time resource loading.
     6→
     7→## Rationale
     8→
     9→Agent skills are not documentation for humans. They are instructions consumed by AI agents that treat every statement as actionable truth. The agentskills.io format was designed around this constraint: minimal metadata for discovery, structured instructions for activation, and on-demand resources for execution.
    10→
    11→Without format compliance:
    12→- Skills are not portable across agent platforms
    13→- Discovery requires loading full skill content, wasting context tokens
    14→- Agents cannot distinguish skills from general documentation
    15→
    16→With format compliance:
    17→- Skills are discoverable from ~100 tokens of frontmatter metadata
    18→- Full activation loads < 500 lines of structured instructions
    19→- Resources load on demand, keeping context lean during execution
    20→
    21→## Format Structure
    22→
    23→### Required: SKILL.md
    24→
    25→The minimum viable skill is a single `SKILL.md` file containing:
    26→
    27→**Frontmatter** (YAML between `---` markers):
    28→```yaml
    29→---
    30→name: library-name
    31→description: >
    32→  What the skill does and when to use it. Include specific keywords
    33→  for agent discovery. Mention what NOT to use it for.
    34→---
    35→```
    36→
    37→**Frontmatter constraints:**
    38→- `name`: 1-64 characters, lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens, must match parent directory name
    39→- `description`: 1-1024 characters, trigger-optimized for agent matching
    40→
    41→**Body:** Free-form markdown — no structural restrictions, but Skill Forge follows a consistent section order (see skill-sections.md in create-skill/data/).
    42→
    43→### Optional: Supporting Directories
    44→
    45→```
    46→skill-name/
    47→├── SKILL.md              # Instructions and API reference
    48→├── scripts/              # Executable automation
    49→├── references/           # Detailed reference material
    50→└── assets/               # Templates, schemas, configs
    51→```
    52→
    53→All subdirectories are exactly one level deep. Files are loaded on demand when SKILL.md directs — never automatically.
    54→
    55→## Progressive Disclosure Model
    56→
    57→The format implements a three-phase loading model:
    58→
    59→| Phase | What Loads | Token Cost | When |
    60→| --- | --- | --- | --- |
    61→| Discovery | `name` + `description` from frontmatter | ~50-100 tokens | Agent startup, all skills |
    62→| Activation | Full `SKILL.md` body | < 5000 tokens | Task matches skill description |
    63→| Execution | Files from `scripts/`, `references/`, `assets/` | Variable | SKILL.md directs agent to load |
    64→
    65→## Pattern Examples
    66→
    67→### Example 1: Trigger-Optimized Description
    68→
    69→**Context:** Writing a skill description that agents can match to user tasks.
    70→
    71→**Implementation:**
    72→```yaml
    73→description: >
    74→  Extract and transform data from PostgreSQL databases using pg client.
    75→  Use for database queries, schema inspection, connection management,
    76→  and migration execution. NOT for: MongoDB, Redis, or other non-SQL stores.
    77→  NOT for: ORM-level abstractions (use typeorm-skill or prisma-skill instead).
    78→```
    79→
    80→**Key Points:**
    81→- Leads with what the skill does (positive triggers)
    82→- Includes negative triggers to prevent false matches
    83→- References alternative skills for excluded use cases
    84→- Specific keywords: "PostgreSQL", "pg client", "schema inspection"
    85→
    86→### Example 2: Just-in-Time Resource Loading
    87→
    88→**Context:** A skill needs to reference a complex schema during execution.
    89→
    90→**Implementation:**
    91→```markdown
    92→## Configuration
    93→
    94→The library accepts a configuration object matching the schema in
    95→`references/config-schema.md`. Load that file now to validate
    96→the user's configuration against the expected structure.
    97→```
    98→
    99→**Key Points:**
   100→- The directive is explicit: "Load that file now"
   101→- Agents do not pre-load reference files — they follow the instruction when they reach it
   102→- Relative paths use forward slashes, one level deep only
   103→
   104→### Example 3: Procedural Instructions for Agents
   105→
   106→**Context:** Writing skill instructions that agents execute deterministically.
   107→
   108→**Implementation:**
   109→```markdown
   110→## Setup
   111→
   112→1. Check if `pg` is installed: run `npm list pg` in the project directory.
   113→2. If not installed, run `npm install pg`.
   114→3. Locate the database configuration:
   115→   - Check `src/config/database.ts` first
   116→   - If not found, check environment variables: `DATABASE_URL`, `PG_HOST`
   117→   - If neither exists, ask the user for connection details
   118→4. Validate the connection by running the health check in `scripts/health-check.sql`.
   119→```
   120→
   121→**Key Points:**
   122→- Step-by-step numbering with decision trees
   123→- Third-person imperative voice ("Check if..." not "You should check...")
   124→- Concrete file paths and commands, not vague guidance
   125→- Fallback paths explicitly mapped
   126→
   127→### Example 4: Skill Forge Compliance Checks
   128→
   129→**Context:** The validate step in create-skill checks agentskills.io compliance.
   130→
   131→**Implementation:** Validation covers:
   132→1. Frontmatter present with required `name` and `description` fields
   133→2. `name` matches parent directory name and formatting rules
   134→3. `description` length within 1-1024 characters
   135→4. SKILL.md body under 500 lines (warning, not failure)
   136→5. All relative paths resolve to existing files
   137→6. No deeply nested subdirectories (max one level)
   138→
   139→**Key Points:**
   140→- Compliance is checked during create-skill, not as a post-hoc audit
   141→- Line count is a guideline — exceeding 500 lines produces a warning
   142→- Path validation prevents broken references in the published skill
   143→
   144→## Anti-Patterns
   145→
   146→- Writing skills as documentation (README-style) instead of procedural agent instructions
   147→- Deeply nested directory structures — one level maximum from skill root
   148→- Vague descriptions ("helps with databases") — descriptions must be specific and trigger-optimized
   149→- Including redundant content that duplicates what the agent already knows (standard language features, basic CLI commands)
   150→- Bundling large library code inside skills — skills reference existing tools, not replace them
   151→
   152→## Related Fragments
   153→
   154→- [skill-lifecycle.md](skill-lifecycle.md) — where agentskills.io compliance fits in the pipeline
   155→- [confidence-tiers.md](confidence-tiers.md) — how citations appear within the formatted output
   156→- [zero-hallucination.md](zero-hallucination.md) — the integrity principle that shapes skill content
   157→
   158→_Source: synthesized from agentskills.io specification, what-are-skills.mdx, integrate-skills.mdx, and Best Practices for Creating Agent Skills_
   159→
```

**Here's what changed across all 4 files:**

---

### File 1: `src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md`

**Before:** Template started directly with `# {skill_name}` — no frontmatter.

**After:** Template now includes YAML frontmatter block with `name` and `description` fields before the heading, matching agentskills.io spec requirements.

---

### File 2: `src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md`

**Before:** Section 2 "Assemble SKILL.md" jumped straight to required sections (Overview, Description, etc.) with no mention of frontmatter.

**After:** Section 2 now leads with a **"Frontmatter (REQUIRED)" block** — explicit instructions to generate `name` (lowercase, must match directory) and `description` (trigger-optimized, max 1024 chars) before the body sections.

---

### File 3: `src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-05-validate.md`

**Before:** Section 1 only checked body structure (Overview, Description, Key Exports, Usage Patterns). No frontmatter validation at all.

**After:** New **section 1 "Validate SKILL.md Frontmatter"** added — checks frontmatter presence, `name`/`description` fields, name-directory match, no unknown fields. Explicitly warns that missing frontmatter will fail `npx skills add` and `skills-ref validate`. Former section 1 renumbered to section 2. All subsequent sections renumbered (3-6).

---

### File 4: `src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md`

**Before:** After validating SKILL.md exists (step 2), went straight to loading forge tier state (step 3).

**After:** New **step 3 "Validate Frontmatter Compliance"** inserted between file existence check and tier loading. Checks same frontmatter rules — catches non-compliant skills from any workflow. Issues logged as pre-check findings for the gap report. Subsequent steps renumbered (4-8).

---

Here's the assessment:

**Create-skill (Forge/Deep) already generates frontmatter** — but there's a **spec compliance issue**:

The create-skill compile step (line 63-71) and skill-sections.md (line 7-14) use these frontmatter fields:
```yaml
name: {brief.name}
version: {brief.version}
description: {skill-name} — {count} verified functions
author: {source-author}
```

**Problem:** `version` and `author` are **not permitted** by the agentskills.io spec. Only 6 fields are allowed: `name`, `description`, `license`, `compatibility`, `metadata`, `allowed-tools`. Unknown fields cause `skills-ref validate` to fail.

Additionally, the validate step (step-06) relies entirely on `skills_ref` tool availability — if unavailable, validation is silently skipped with no fallback check.

Let me also check create-stack-skill.

Perfect! I found the information you need. Here's the exact frontmatter structure and fields for stack skills:

## Compile Step
The compile step is in `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-06-compile-stack.md`

This is **Step 6: Compile Stack Skill** - it:
- Loads the stack-skill-template structure (defined in the template file)
- Compiles the integration layer (cross-cutting patterns + library pair integrations)
- Compiles per-library sections
- Presents the compiled SKILL.md for user review before writing

## SKILL.md Frontmatter Fields

The SKILL.md frontmatter structure uses these fields (from the template at `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/data/stack-skill-template.md`):

1. **Header line:** `# {project_name} Stack Skill`
2. **Tagline:** `> {lib_count} libraries | {integration_count} integration patterns | Forge tier: {tier}`

The main SKILL.md sections (not traditional YAML frontmatter, but structured metadata):
- **Integration Patterns** (Cross-cutting patterns + Library pair integrations)
- **Library Reference Index** (table with Library, Imports, Key Exports, Confidence, Reference)
- **Per-Library Summaries** (with Role, Key exports, Usage pattern, Confidence fields)
- **Conventions** (Project-specific conventions)

Additionally, there's a **metadata.json** with formal frontmatter containing:
- `skill_type`: "stack"
- `skill_name`: "{project}-stack"
- `version`: "1.0.0"
- `created`: "{date}"
- `forge_tier`: "Quick|Forge|Deep"
- `library_count`: number
- `integration_count`: number
- `libraries`: array of library names
- `integration_pairs`: array of [lib1, lib2] pairs
- `confidence_distribution`: object with T1, T1-low, T2 counts

All content sections include **confidence tier labels** (T1, T1-low, or T2) as a key requirement.

    42→
    43→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    44→
    45→- From step 04: per_library_extractions[] with exports, patterns, confidence
    46→- From step 05: integration_graph with pairs, types, hub libraries, cross-cutting patterns
    47→- This step produces: skill_content (compiled SKILL.md ready for writing)
    48→- User interaction: Gate 2 — compile checkpoint required
    49→
    50→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    51→
    52→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    53→
    54→### 1. Load Template Structure
    55→
    56→Load `{stackSkillTemplate}` and prepare SKILL.md section structure.
    57→
    58→### 2. Compile Integration Layer
    59→
    60→**This is the core value of the stack skill.** Compile in order:
    61→
    62→**Cross-cutting patterns** (if any):
    63→- Patterns spanning 3+ libraries
    64→- Middleware chains, shared configuration, common architectural patterns
    65→
    66→**Library pair integrations:**
    67→- For each detected integration pair from step 05:
    68→  - Type classification
    69→  - Pattern description with file:line citations
    70→  - Key files demonstrating the integration
    71→  - Confidence tier label
    72→
    73→**Hub library connections:**
    74→- For each hub library (3+ connections):
    75→  - Role in the stack architecture
    76→  - How it connects to partner libraries
    77→
    78→### 3. Compile Per-Library Sections
    79→
    80→For each confirmed library (ordered by integration connectivity, then import count):
    81→
    82→- Role in stack (one-line description)
    83→- Key exports used in this project
    84→- Usage patterns from extraction
    85→- Confidence tier label
    86→- Link to reference file: `./references/{library}.md`
    87→
    88→### 4. Compile Project Conventions
    89→
    90→Extract project-specific conventions from the extractions:
    91→- Common initialization patterns
    92→- Error handling approaches across libraries
    93→- Configuration conventions
    94→- Import organization patterns
    95→
    96→### 5. Compile Library Reference Index
    97→
    98→Create the reference index table:
    99→
   100→| Library | Imports | Key Exports | Confidence | Reference |
   101→|---------|---------|-------------|------------|-----------|
   102→| ... | ... | ... | ... | ... |
   103→
   104→### 6. Present Compiled SKILL.md Preview
   105→
   106→"**Stack skill compilation complete. Please review:**
   107→
   108→---
   109→
   110→{Display full compiled SKILL.md content}
   111→
   112→---
   113→
   114→**Compilation stats:**
   115→- **Libraries:** {count}
   116→- **Integration pairs:** {count}
   117→- **Cross-cutting patterns:** {count}
   118→- **Confidence:** T1: {count}, T1-low: {count}, T2: {count}
   119→
   120→**Please review the integration layer and per-library sections.**
   121→- Does the integration layer capture how your libraries connect?
   122→- Are the per-library summaries accurate?
   123→- Any sections to adjust before writing output?"
   124→
   125→### 7. Present MENU OPTIONS
   126→
   127→Display: **Select:** [C] Continue to Output Generation
   128→
   129→#### EXECUTION RULES:
   130→
   131→- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting compilation
   132→- ONLY proceed to next step when user approves and selects 'C'
   133→
   134→#### Menu Handling Logic:
   135→
   136→- IF C: Store skill_content, then load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
   137→- IF Any other: Process as feedback, adjust compilation, redisplay preview, then [Redisplay Menu Options](#7-present-menu-options)
   138→
   139→---
   140→
   141→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   142→
   143→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   144→
   145→- SKILL.md follows stack-skill-template structure
   146→- Integration layer presented FIRST (primary value)
   147→- All content includes confidence tier labels
   148→- Per-library sections ordered by connectivity
   149→- User reviewed and approved compilation
   150→- skill_content stored for step 07
   151→

   153→
   154→- Integration layer missing or buried below library listings
   155→- Content without confidence tier labels
   156→- Proceeding without user review (Gate 2)
   157→- Writing files in this step (step 07's job)
   158→- Fabricating integration patterns not from step 05
   159→
   160→**Master Rule:** Integration layer is the value. Present it first, get user approval before writing.

  "new_string": "### 2. Validate SKILL.md Frontmatter (agentskills.io Compliance)\n\nVerify SKILL.md has valid YAML frontmatter — REQUIRED for ecosystem compatibility:\n\n- [ ] Frontmatter present — file starts with `---` delimiter and has closing `---`\n- [ ] `name` field — present, non-empty, lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens only, 1-64 chars\n- [ ] `name` matches skill output directory name (`{project_name}-stack`)\n- [ ] `description` field — present, non-empty, 1-1024 characters\n- [ ] No unknown fields — only `name`, `description`, `license`, `compatibility`, `metadata`, `allowed-tools` are permitted\n\nRecord frontmatter violations as **WARNING** findings. Skills without valid frontmatter will fail `npx skills add` and `skills-ref validate`.\n\n### 3. Validate SKILL.md Body Structure\n\nLoad `{stackSkillTemplate}` and verify SKILL.md contains expected sections:\n\n- [ ] Header with project name, library count, integration count, forge tier\n- [ ] Integration Patterns section (should appear BEFORE per-library summaries)\n- [ ] Library Reference Index table\n- [ ] Per-Library Summaries section\n- [ ] Conventions section\n\nRecord missing sections as **WARNING** findings."
}

## Comprehensive Frontmatter Compliance Audit Report

I've thoroughly analyzed the downstream impacts of frontmatter compliance changes across the bmad-module-skill-forge codebase. Here are my findings:

---

### **FINDING 1: CRITICAL — Incorrect Frontmatter Requirements in step-06-validate.md**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-06-validate.md`

**Lines:** 70

**Issue:** Line 70 lists `version` and `author` as required frontmatter fields, which contradicts the spec:

```markdown
Line 70: - Frontmatter contains required fields (name, version, description, author)
```

**What it should say:**
```markdown
- Frontmatter contains required fields (name, description)
```

**Impact:** The validation step will check for fields that are explicitly forbidden by the agentskills.io specification. This creates a compliance bug where skills would fail validation if they correctly follow the spec (no version/author in frontmatter), or pass validation while violating the spec (if they include version/author in frontmatter).

**Related correct reference:** Lines 93-94 in the same file correctly state that `version` and `author` should NOT be in frontmatter.

---

### **FINDING 2: Markdown Anchor Reference Error in step-06-compile-stack.md**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-06-compile-stack.md`

**Line:** 158

**Issue:** The Menu Handling Logic references an outdated section anchor:

```markdown
Line 158: - IF Any other: Process as feedback, adjust compilation, redisplay preview, then [Redisplay Menu Options](#7-present-menu-options)
```

**What it should reference:**
```markdown
- IF Any other: Process as feedback, adjust compilation, redisplay preview, then [Redisplay Menu Options](#8-present-menu-options)
```

**Why:** The "Present MENU OPTIONS" section is ### 8 (line 146), not ### 7. Section ### 7 is "Present Compiled SKILL.md Preview" (line 125).

**Impact:** Broken internal documentation anchor — users/agents following the documentation will be directed to the wrong section when re-displaying menu options.

---

### **FINDING 3: Workflows Correctly Handle Frontmatter**

**Status:** GOOD — No issues found

**Coverage verified:**
- **update-skill workflow:** Steps 03 (re-extract), 04 (merge), and 06 (write) do NOT strip or regenerate frontmatter. The merge step (04) explicitly preserves [MANUAL] sections and treats frontmatter as part of the existing skill structure. ✓ Correct
- **audit-skill workflow:** Steps 03 (structural-diff) and 05 (severity-classify) perform structural analysis and semantic drift detection. They do NOT validate frontmatter compliance — that's scope of create-skill and export-skill. ✓ Correct
- **export-skill workflow:** Step 01 (load-skill) validates spec compliance, including checking frontmatter for required `name` and `description` fields. It does not attempt to use frontmatter `version`/`author` — correctly reads those from `metadata.json`. ✓ Correct

---

### **FINDING 4: agentskills-spec.md Is Correct**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/agentskills-spec.md`

**Status:** CLEAN — No contradictions found

**Example 4 (lines 127-142)** correctly documents the five validation checks:
1. Frontmatter present with required `name` and `description` fields
2. `name` matches parent directory name and formatting rules
3. `description` length within 1-1024 characters
4. SKILL.md body under 500 lines
5. All relative paths resolve to existing files

It does NOT mention version/author as frontmatter fields. ✓ Correct

---

### **FINDING 5: Correct References in Workflow Documentation**

**Files validated:**
- `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-05-compile.md` (line 79) — Correctly states: "Only `name` and `description` in frontmatter — `version` and `author` go in metadata.json" ✓
- `src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md` (line 23) — Correctly states: "`version` and `author` belong in metadata.json, NOT in frontmatter" ✓
- `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-skill.md` (line 91) — Correctly lists `version` as a metadata.json field, not frontmatter ✓

---

## Summary Table

| Finding | Severity | File | Lines | Type | Action Required |
|---------|----------|------|-------|------|-----------------|
| 1 | CRITICAL | `create-skill/steps-c/step-06-validate.md` | 70 | Spec Contradiction | Remove `version` and `author` from required frontmatter fields list |
| 2 | HIGH | `create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-06-compile-stack.md` | 158 | Broken Anchor | Change `#7-present-menu-options` to `#8-present-menu-options` |
| 3 | NONE | All workflow steps | — | Compliance | Workflows correctly preserve and validate frontmatter |
| 4 | NONE | `agentskills-spec.md` | 127-142 | Spec Clarity | Example 4 is correct and complete |
| 5 | NONE | Multiple files | 23, 79, 91 | Documentation | Related files are consistent and correct |

---

## Recommended Priority

1. **Fix Finding 1 immediately** — This causes validation to fail skills that are spec-compliant
2. **Fix Finding 2 in next documentation pass** — Improves user experience, but not critical to functionality

     1→# Module Standards
     2→
     3→**Purpose:** Defines what a BMAD module is, its structure, and the three types of modules.
     4→
     5→---
     6→
     7→## What is a BMAD Module?
     8→
     9→A **BMAD module** is a self-contained package of functionality that extends the BMAD framework. Modules provide:
    10→- **Agents** — AI personas with specialized expertise and menu-driven commands
    11→- **Workflows** — Structured processes for accomplishing complex tasks
    12→- **Configuration** — module.yaml for user customization
    13→
    14→---
    15→
    16→## Module Types
    17→
    18→### 1. Standalone Module
    19→
    20→A new, independent module focused on a specific domain.
    21→
    22→**Characteristics:**
    23→- Own module code (e.g., `healthcare-ai`, `legal-assist`)
    24→- Independent of other modules
    25→- Can be installed alongside any other modules
    26→- Has its own agents, workflows, configuration
    27→
    28→**Location:** `src/modules/{module-code}/`
    29→
    30→**Example:** CIS (Creative Innovation Suite) — a standalone module for innovation workflows
    31→
    32→---
    33→
    34→### 2. Extension Module
    35→
    36→Extends an existing BMAD module with additional functionality.
    37→
    38→**Characteristics:**
    39→- Builds upon an existing module's agents and workflows
    40→- May add new agents or workflows that complement the base module
    41→- Shares configuration context with the extended module
    42→- Typically installed alongside the module it extends
    43→
    44→**Location:** `src/modules/{base-module}/extensions/{extension-code}/`
    45→
    46→**Example:** An extension to BMM that adds specialized security review workflows
    47→
    48→---
    49→
    50→### Extension Module: Override & Merge Pattern
    51→
    52→When an extension module is installed, its files merge with the base module following these rules:
    53→
    54→#### Code Matching
    55→
    56→The extension's `module.yaml` `code:` field matches the base module's code:
    57→
    58→```yaml
    59→# Base module: src/modules/bmm/module.yaml
    60→code: bmm
    61→
    62→# Extension: src/modules/bmm/extensions/security/module.yaml
    63→code: bmm  # SAME CODE — extends BMM
    64→```
    65→
    66→The **folder name** is unique (e.g., `bmm-security`) but the `code:` matches the base module.
    67→
    68→#### File Merge Rules
    69→
    70→| File Type | Same Name | Different Name |
    71→|-----------|-----------|----------------|
    72→| Agent file | **OVERRIDE** — replaces the base agent | **ADD** — new agent added |
    73→| Workflow folder | **OVERRIDE** — replaces the base workflow | **ADD** — new workflow added |
    74→| Other files | **OVERRIDE** — replaces base file | **ADD** — new file added |
    75→
    76→#### Examples
    77→
    78→**Override scenario:**
    79→```
    80→Base module (BMM):
    81→├── agents/
    82→│   └── pm.agent.yaml          # Original PM agent
    83→
    84→Extension (bmm-security):
    85→├── agents/
    86→│   └── pm.agent.yaml          # Security-focused PM — REPLACES original
    87→
    88→Result after installation:
    89→├── agents/
    90→│   └── pm.agent.yaml          # Now the security version
    91→```
    92→
    93→**Add scenario:**
    94→```
    95→Base module (BMM):
    96→├── agents/
    97→│   ├── pm.agent.yaml
    98→│   └── architect.agent.yaml
    99→
   100→Extension (bmm-security):
   101→├── agents/
   102→│   └── security-auditor.agent.yaml  # NEW agent
   103→
   104→Result after installation:
   105→├── agents/
   106→│   ├── pm.agent.yaml
   107→│   ├── architect.agent.yaml
   108→│   └── security-auditor.agent.yaml  # ADDED
   109→```
   110→
   111→**Mixed scenario:**
   112→```
   113→Extension contains both overrides and new files — applies rules per file
   114→```
   115→
   116→---
   117→
   118→### 3. Global Module
   119→
   120→Affects the entire BMAD framework and all modules.
   121→
   122→**Characteristics:**
   123→- Core functionality that impacts all modules
   124→- Often provides foundational services or utilities
   125→- Installed at the framework level
   126→- Use sparingly — only for truly global concerns
   127→
   128→**Location:** `src/modules/{module-code}/` with `global: true` in module.yaml
   129→
   130→**Example:** A module that provides universal logging or telemetry across BMAD
   131→
   132→---
   133→
   134→## Required Module Structure
   135→
   136→```
   137→{module-code}/
   138→├── module.yaml                 # Module configuration (REQUIRED)
   139→├── README.md                   # Module documentation (REQUIRED)
   140→├── agents/                     # Agent definitions (if any)
   141→│   └── {agent-name}.agent.yaml
   142→├── workflows/                  # Workflow definitions (if any)
   143→│   └── {workflow-name}/
   144→│       └── workflow.md
   145→└── {other folders}             # Tasks, templates, data as needed
   146→```
   147→
   148→---
   149→
   150→## Required Files
   151→
   152→### module.yaml (REQUIRED)
   153→
   154→Every module MUST have a `module.yaml` file with at minimum:
   155→
   156→```yaml
   157→code: {module-code}
   158→name: "Module Display Name"
   159→header: "Brief module description"
   160→subheader: "Additional context"
   161→default_selected: false
   162→```
   163→
   164→See: `module-yaml-conventions.md` for full specification.
   165→
   166→---
   167→
   168→### README.md (REQUIRED)
   169→
   170→Every module MUST have a README.md with:
   171→- Module name and purpose
   172→- Installation instructions
   173→- Components section (agents, workflows)
   174→- Quick start guide
   175→- Module structure diagram
   176→- Configuration section
   177→- Usage examples
   178→- Author information
   179→
   180→---
   181→
   182→## Optional Components
   183→
   184→### Agents
   185→
   186→Agents are AI personas with:
   187→- Metadata (id, name, title, icon, module)
   188→- Persona (role, identity, communication_style, principles)
   189→- Menu (trigger → workflow/exec mappings)
   190→
   191→See: `agent-architecture.md` for design guidance.
   192→
   193→---
   194→
   195→### Workflows
   196→
   197→Workflows are structured processes with:
   198→- workflow.md (entry point)
   199→- steps/ folder with step files
   200→- data/ folder with shared reference
   201→- templates/ folder if needed
   202→
   203→---
   204→
   205→---
   206→
   207→## Module Type Decision Tree
   208→
   209→```
   210→START: Creating a module
   211→│
   212→├─ Is this a brand new independent domain?
   213→│  └─ YES → Standalone Module
   214→│
   215→├─ Does this extend an existing module?
   216→│  └─ YES → Extension Module
   217→│
   218→└─ Does this affect all modules globally?
   219→   └─ YES → Global Module (use sparingly)
   220→```
   221→
   222→---
   223→
   224→## Naming Conventions
   225→
   226→### Module Code
   227→
   228→- **kebab-case** (e.g., `bmm`, `cis`, `bmgd`, `healthcare-ai`)
   229→- Short, memorable, descriptive
   230→- 2-20 characters
   231→- Lowercase letters, numbers, hyphens only
   232→
   233→### Agent Files
   234→
   235→- Format: `{role-name}.agent.yaml`
   236→- Example: `pm.agent.yaml`, `architect.agent.yaml`
   237→
   238→### Workflow Folders
   239→
   240→- Format: `{workflow-name}/`
   241→- Example: `prd/`, `create-architecture/`
   242→
   243→---
   244→
   245→## Module Dependencies
   246→
   247→Modules can depend on:
   248→- **Core BMAD** — Always available
   249→- **Other modules** — Specify in module.yaml as `dependencies:`
   250→- **External tools** — Document in README
   251→
   252→---
   253→
   254→## Quick Reference
   255→
   256→| Question | Answer |
   257→|----------|--------|
   258→| What's a module? | Self-contained package of agents, workflows, config |
   259→| What are the types? | Standalone, Extension, Global |
   260→| What's required? | module.yaml, README.md |
   261→| Where do modules live? | `src/modules/{code}/` |
   262→| How do agents work? | Menu triggers → workflow/exec |
   263→| How does installation work? | module.yaml prompts |
   264→
```

Excellent. I now have a complete picture of the skills-ref Python project. Let me compile a comprehensive report.

## Complete Analysis of skills-ref Python Project

I've thoroughly explored the entire skills-ref Python project. Here's the COMPLETE picture you need for your Node.js port:

### 1. PROJECT METADATA & DEPENDENCIES

**pyproject.toml:**
- Project: `skills-ref` v0.1.0
- Python: `>=3.11`
- Dependencies:
  - `click>=8.0` (CLI framework)
  - `strictyaml>=1.7.3` (YAML parsing)
- Dev dependencies: `pytest>=7.0`, `ruff>=0.8.0`
- Entry point: `skills-ref = "skills_ref.cli:main"` (Click group with subcommands)

### 2. MODULE STRUCTURE

```
src/skills_ref/
├── __init__.py          (Public API exports)
├── cli.py               (CLI commands via Click)
├── errors.py            (Exception classes)
├── models.py            (SkillProperties dataclass)
├── parser.py            (Frontmatter parsing)
├── prompt.py            (XML generation)
└── validator.py         (Validation logic - THE CORE)
```

### 3. CLI COMMANDS (3 commands total)

#### Command 1: `skills-ref validate <skill_path>`
- **Purpose**: Validates a skill directory
- **Input**: Path to skill directory or directly to SKILL.md file
- **Exit codes**: 0 (valid), 1 (errors found)
- **Output**: Prints validation errors to stderr, success message to stdout
- **Logic**:
  1. If path is a file named SKILL.md (case-insensitive), resolve to parent directory
  2. Call `validate(skill_path)` which returns list of error strings
  3. If errors exist, print each with "  - " prefix to stderr and exit(1)
  4. If no errors, print "Valid skill: {path}"

#### Command 2: `skills-ref read-properties <skill_path>`
- **Purpose**: Read and output skill frontmatter as JSON
- **Input**: Path to skill directory or directly to SKILL.md file
- **Exit codes**: 0 (success), 1 (parse error)
- **Output**: JSON dump of SkillProperties.to_dict() to stdout
- **Logic**:
  1. If path is a file named SKILL.md, resolve to parent directory
  2. Call `read_properties(skill_path)` which returns SkillProperties
  3. Convert to dict via `.to_dict()` and output as JSON (indent=2)
  4. If error, print error message to stderr and exit(1)

#### Command 3: `skills-ref to-prompt <skill_path1> [<skill_path2> ...]`
- **Purpose**: Generate `<available_skills>` XML block for agent system prompts
- **Input**: One or more skill paths
- **Exit codes**: 0 (success), 1 (error)
- **Output**: Multi-line XML string to stdout
- **Logic**:
  1. For each path: if it's SKILL.md file, resolve to parent directory
  2. Call `to_prompt([resolved_paths])`
  3. Print output to stdout

### 4. FULL VALIDATION LOGIC (the heart of it)

#### **Frontmatter Parsing** (`parser.py`)

**Function: `parse_frontmatter(content: str) -> tuple[dict, str]`**

1. **Check frontmatter start**: Content MUST start with `---`
   - Error: `"SKILL.md must start with YAML frontmatter (---)"`

2. **Split on `---`**: Split content by `---` with maxsplit=2
   - Error if len(parts) < 3: `"SKILL.md frontmatter not properly closed with ---"`

3. **Parse YAML**: Use strictyaml.load() on parts[1]
   - Catches `strictyaml.YAMLError` → Error: `"Invalid YAML in frontmatter: {error}"`

4. **Validate structure**: Parsed result must be a dict
   - Error: `"SKILL.md frontmatter must be a YAML mapping"`

5. **Normalize metadata field**: If "metadata" key exists and is a dict, convert all keys/values to strings

6. **Return**: tuple of (metadata dict, body string)

**Function: `find_skill_md(skill_dir: Path) -> Optional[Path]`**
- Looks for SKILL.md first (preferred), then skill.md
- Case-insensitive search
- Returns None if not found

**Function: `read_properties(skill_dir: Path) -> SkillProperties`**
- Finds SKILL.md (error: `"SKILL.md not found in {dir}"`)
- Parses frontmatter
- **Requires name**: Error: `"Missing required field in frontmatter: name"`
- **Requires description**: Error: `"Missing required field in frontmatter: description"`
- **Name validation**: Must be non-empty string, error: `"Field 'name' must be a non-empty string"`
- **Description validation**: Must be non-empty string, error: `"Field 'description' must be a non-empty string"`
- Returns SkillProperties instance

#### **Validation** (`validator.py`)

**Constants:**
- `MAX_SKILL_NAME_LENGTH = 64`
- `MAX_DESCRIPTION_LENGTH = 1024`
- `MAX_COMPATIBILITY_LENGTH = 500`
- `ALLOWED_FIELDS = {"name", "description", "license", "allowed-tools", "metadata", "compatibility"}`

**Function: `validate(skill_dir: Path) -> list[str]`**

Entry point that performs ALL validation. Returns list of error strings (empty = valid).

Checks performed in order:
1. **Path exists**: `"Path does not exist: {path}"`
2. **Is directory**: `"Not a directory: {path}"`
3. **Find SKILL.md**: `"Missing required file: SKILL.md"`
4. **Parse frontmatter**: Catches ParseError → returns `[str(e)]`
5. **Validate metadata**: Calls `validate_metadata(metadata, skill_dir)`

**Function: `validate_metadata(metadata: dict, skill_dir: Optional[Path]) -> list[str]`**

Core validation logic:

1. **Unknown fields check** (via `_validate_metadata_fields`):
   - Error: `"Unexpected fields in frontmatter: {fields}. Only {allowed} are allowed."`

2. **Name validation** (via `_validate_name`):
   - Missing: `"Missing required field in frontmatter: name"`
   - Not string/empty: `"Field 'name' must be a non-empty string"`
   - NFKC normalization applied before checks
   - Length > 64: `"Skill name '{name}' exceeds 64 character limit ({len} chars)"`
   - Not lowercase: `"Skill name '{name}' must be lowercase"`
   - Starts/ends with hyphen: `"Skill name cannot start or end with a hyphen"`
   - Has `--`: `"Skill name cannot contain consecutive hyphens"`
   - Invalid chars (only alphanumeric + hyphen allowed): `"Skill name '{name}' contains invalid characters. Only letters, digits, and hyphens are allowed."`
   - Directory mismatch (after NFKC normalization): `"Directory name '{dir}' must match skill name '{name}'"`

3. **Description validation** (via `_validate_description`):
   - Missing: `"Missing required field in frontmatter: description"`
   - Not string/empty: `"Field 'description' must be a non-empty string"`
   - Length > 1024: `"Description exceeds 1024 character limit ({len} chars)"`

4. **Compatibility validation** (via `_validate_compatibility`, only if present):
   - Not string: `"Field 'compatibility' must be a string"`
   - Length > 500: `"Compatibility exceeds 500 character limit ({len} chars)"`

**Important details:**
- All i18n characters (Unicode letters) are allowed in names
- NFKC normalization: applies to both name and directory name before comparison
- Hyphens allowed but NOT at start/end, NOT consecutive
- Only alphanumeric + hyphens pass validation (after i18n characters)
- Field names in YAML are case-sensitive (use `"allowed-tools"` NOT `"allowed_tools"`)

### 5. DATA MODEL

**SkillProperties** (dataclass):
```python
name: str                          # Required, kebab-case
description: str                   # Required
license: Optional[str] = None
compatibility: Optional[str] = None
allowed_tools: Optional[str] = None  # Key: "allowed-tools" in YAML
metadata: dict[str, str] = {}

to_dict() -> dict:  # Excludes None values, maps allowed_tools to "allowed-tools"
```

### 6. PROMPT GENERATION (`prompt.py`)

**Function: `to_prompt(skill_dirs: list[Path]) -> str`**

Generates XML block:
```xml
<available_skills>
<skill>
<name>skill-name</name>
<description>Description text</description>
<location>/absolute/path/to/SKILL.md</location>
</skill>
</available_skills>
```

Details:
- Resolves paths to absolute
- Calls `read_properties()` for each skill (validates it exists/parses)
- **HTML escapes** both name and description (uses `html.escape()`)
- Location is absolute path to SKILL.md file
- Empty list returns: `"<available_skills>\n</available_skills>"`

### 7. ERROR HIERARCHY

```
SkillError (base)
├── ParseError (SKILL.md parsing failures)
└── ValidationError (semantic validation failures)
    └── Has .errors attribute: list[str]
```

### 8. TEST COVERAGE (comprehensive)

**Parser tests** cover:
- Valid frontmatter
- Missing/unclosed frontmatter
- Invalid YAML
- Non-dict frontmatter
- Missing SKILL.md, name, description
- Case-insensitive SKILL.md lookup (prefers SKILL.md)
- Metadata field parsing

**Validator tests** cover:
- Valid skills (minimal and with all fields)
- Path validation (nonexistent, not directory)
- Missing SKILL.md
- All name errors (case, length, hyphens, chars, directory match)
- Description length
- Compatibility length
- Unknown fields
- i18n support (Chinese, Russian, Arabic detection via lowercase check)
- NFKC normalization with composed/decomposed characters

**Prompt tests** cover:
- Empty list
- Single skill
- Multiple skills
- XML special character escaping (`<`, `>`, `&`)

### 9. KEY IMPLEMENTATION DETAILS FOR NODE.JS PORT

1. **Unicode handling**: Use NFKC normalization (JavaScript's `String.prototype.normalize("NFKC")`)
2. **YAML parsing**: Strict YAML (no implicit type conversion)
3. **HTML escaping**: Escape `<`, `>`, `&` in XML output
4. **Case-insensitive file lookup**: Check for both SKILL.md and skill.md
5. **Error messages**: Must match EXACTLY for CLI compatibility
6. **Exit codes**: Always 0 or 1
7. **Output streams**: Use stderr for errors, stdout for normal output/results
8. **Path resolution**: Support both directory and file (SKILL.md) inputs
9. **Validation order**: Check structure first (file exists, is dir, etc.), then content
10. **Allowed fields are strict**: Any field not in ALLOWED_FIELDS is an error

All 26 files and functions are fully documented above. You have everything needed for a complete Node.js port.

{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/tools/skill-check/lib/parser.js",
  "content": "'use strict';\n\nconst fs = require('node:fs');\nconst path = require('node:path');\nconst yaml = require('js-yaml');\n\nclass ParseError extends Error {\n  constructor(message) {\n    super(message);\n    this.name = 'ParseError';\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Find SKILL.md in a directory (case-insensitive, prefers SKILL.md).\n * @param {string} skillDir - Path to skill directory\n * @returns {string|null} Path to SKILL.md or null\n */\nfunction findSkillMd(skillDir) {\n  let entries;\n  try {\n    entries = fs.readdirSync(skillDir);\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  // Prefer SKILL.md, fall back to skill.md\n  const preferred = entries.find((e) => e === 'SKILL.md');\n  if (preferred) return path.join(skillDir, preferred);\n\n  const fallback = entries.find((e) => e.toLowerCase() === 'skill.md');\n  if (fallback) return path.join(skillDir, fallback);\n\n  return null;\n}\n\n/**\n * Parse YAML frontmatter from SKILL.md content.\n * @param {string} content - Raw file content\n * @returns {{ metadata: object, body: string }}\n * @throws {ParseError}\n */\nfunction parseFrontmatter(content) {\n  if (!content.startsWith('---')) {\n    throw new ParseError('SKILL.md must start with YAML frontmatter (---)');\n  }\n\n  // Split on --- (max 3 parts: empty before first ---, frontmatter, body)\n  const marker = '---';\n  const firstEnd = content.indexOf(marker, 3);\n  if (firstEnd === -1) {\n    throw new ParseError('SKILL.md frontmatter not properly closed with ---');\n  }\n\n  const rawYaml = content.slice(3, firstEnd);\n  const body = content.slice(firstEnd + 3);\n\n  let metadata;\n  try {\n    metadata = yaml.load(rawYaml);\n  } catch (error) {\n    throw new ParseError(`Invalid YAML in frontmatter: ${error.message}`);\n  }\n\n  if (metadata === null || metadata === undefined || typeof metadata !== 'object' || Array.isArray(metadata)) {\n    throw new ParseError('SKILL.md frontmatter must be a YAML mapping');\n  }\n\n  // Normalize metadata field: convert all keys/values to strings\n  if (metadata.metadata && typeof metadata.metadata === 'object') {\n    const normalized = {};\n    for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(metadata.metadata)) {\n      normalized[String(key)] = String(value);\n    }\n    metadata.metadata = normalized;\n  }\n\n  return { metadata, body };\n}\n\n/**\n * Resolve a skill path — if it's a SKILL.md file, return the parent directory.\n * @param {string} inputPath\n * @returns {string}\n */\nfunction resolveSkillPath(inputPath) {\n  const resolved = path.resolve(inputPath);\n  const basename = path.basename(resolved);\n  if (basename.toLowerCase() === 'skill.md') {\n    return path.dirname(resolved);\n  }\n  return resolved;\n}\n\nmodule.exports = { ParseError, findSkillMd, parseFrontmatter, resolveSkillPath };\n"
}

  "content": "'use strict';\n\nconst fs = require('node:fs');\nconst { ParseError, findSkillMd, parseFrontmatter } = require('./parser');\n\n/**\n * Skill properties extracted from SKILL.md frontmatter.\n */\nclass SkillProperties {\n  constructor({ name, description, license, compatibility, allowedTools, metadata }) {\n    this.name = name;\n    this.description = description;\n    this.license = license || null;\n    this.compatibility = compatibility || null;\n    this.allowedTools = allowedTools || null;\n    this.metadata = metadata || {};\n  }\n\n  toDict() {\n    const result = {\n      name: this.name,\n      description: this.description,\n    };\n    if (this.license !== null) result.license = this.license;\n    if (this.compatibility !== null) result.compatibility = this.compatibility;\n    if (this.allowedTools !== null) result['allowed-tools'] = this.allowedTools;\n    if (Object.keys(this.metadata).length > 0) result.metadata = this.metadata;\n    return result;\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Read and parse skill properties from a skill directory.\n * @param {string} skillDir - Path to skill directory\n * @returns {SkillProperties}\n * @throws {ParseError}\n */\nfunction readProperties(skillDir) {\n  const skillMdPath = findSkillMd(skillDir);\n  if (!skillMdPath) {\n    throw new ParseError(`SKILL.md not found in ${skillDir}`);\n  }\n\n  const content = fs.readFileSync(skillMdPath, 'utf-8');\n  const { metadata } = parseFrontmatter(content);\n\n  if (!metadata.name) {\n    throw new ParseError('Missing required field in frontmatter: name');\n  }\n  if (!metadata.description) {\n    throw new ParseError('Missing required field in frontmatter: description');\n  }\n\n  if (typeof metadata.name !== 'string' || metadata.name.trim() === '') {\n    throw new ParseError(\"Field 'name' must be a non-empty string\");\n  }\n  if (typeof metadata.description !== 'string' || metadata.description.trim() === '') {\n    throw new ParseError(\"Field 'description' must be a non-empty string\");\n  }\n\n  return new SkillProperties({\n    name: metadata.name,\n    description: metadata.description,\n    license: metadata.license || null,\n    compatibility: metadata.compatibility || null,\n    allowedTools: metadata['allowed-tools'] || null,\n    metadata: metadata.metadata || {},\n  });\n}\n\nmodule.exports = { SkillProperties, readProperties };\n"
}

  "content": "'use strict';\n\nconst fs = require('node:fs');\nconst path = require('node:path');\nconst { ParseError, findSkillMd, parseFrontmatter } = require('./parser');\n\nconst MAX_SKILL_NAME_LENGTH = 64;\nconst MAX_DESCRIPTION_LENGTH = 1024;\nconst MAX_COMPATIBILITY_LENGTH = 500;\n\nconst ALLOWED_FIELDS = new Set(['name', 'description', 'license', 'allowed-tools', 'metadata', 'compatibility']);\n\n/**\n * Validate a skill directory against the agentskills.io specification.\n * @param {string} skillDir - Path to skill directory\n * @returns {string[]} List of error messages (empty = valid)\n */\nfunction validate(skillDir) {\n  if (!fs.existsSync(skillDir)) {\n    return [`Path does not exist: ${skillDir}`];\n  }\n\n  const stat = fs.statSync(skillDir);\n  if (!stat.isDirectory()) {\n    return [`Not a directory: ${skillDir}`];\n  }\n\n  const skillMdPath = findSkillMd(skillDir);\n  if (!skillMdPath) {\n    return ['Missing required file: SKILL.md'];\n  }\n\n  const content = fs.readFileSync(skillMdPath, 'utf-8');\n\n  let metadata;\n  try {\n    ({ metadata } = parseFrontmatter(content));\n  } catch (error) {\n    if (error instanceof ParseError) {\n      return [error.message];\n    }\n    throw error;\n  }\n\n  return validateMetadata(metadata, skillDir);\n}\n\n/**\n * Validate frontmatter metadata against the agentskills.io specification.\n * @param {object} metadata - Parsed frontmatter\n * @param {string|null} skillDir - Skill directory path (for name matching)\n * @returns {string[]} List of error messages\n */\nfunction validateMetadata(metadata, skillDir) {\n  const errors = [];\n\n  errors.push(...validateMetadataFields(metadata));\n  errors.push(...validateName(metadata, skillDir));\n  errors.push(...validateDescription(metadata));\n  errors.push(...validateCompatibility(metadata));\n\n  return errors;\n}\n\n/**\n * Check for unknown frontmatter fields.\n */\nfunction validateMetadataFields(metadata) {\n  const unknown = Object.keys(metadata).filter((key) => !ALLOWED_FIELDS.has(key));\n  if (unknown.length > 0) {\n    const allowed = [...ALLOWED_FIELDS].sort().join(', ');\n    return [`Unexpected fields in frontmatter: ${unknown.join(', ')}. Only ${allowed} are allowed.`];\n  }\n  return [];\n}\n\n/**\n * Validate the name field.\n */\nfunction validateName(metadata, skillDir) {\n  const errors = [];\n\n  if (metadata.name === undefined || metadata.name === null) {\n    return ['Missing required field in frontmatter: name'];\n  }\n\n  if (typeof metadata.name !== 'string' || metadata.name.trim() === '') {\n    return [\"Field 'name' must be a non-empty string\"];\n  }\n\n  const name = metadata.name.normalize('NFKC');\n\n  if (name.length > MAX_SKILL_NAME_LENGTH) {\n    errors.push(`Skill name '${name}' exceeds 64 character limit (${name.length} chars)`);\n  }\n\n  if (name !== name.toLowerCase()) {\n    errors.push(`Skill name '${name}' must be lowercase`);\n  }\n\n  if (name.startsWith('-') || name.endsWith('-')) {\n    errors.push('Skill name cannot start or end with a hyphen');\n  }\n\n  if (name.includes('--')) {\n    errors.push('Skill name cannot contain consecutive hyphens');\n  }\n\n  // Only alphanumeric + hyphens allowed\n  if (!/^[\\da-z-]+$/.test(name)) {\n    errors.push(\n      `Skill name '${name}' contains invalid characters. Only letters, digits, and hyphens are allowed.`,\n    );\n  }\n\n  // Name must match directory name\n  if (skillDir) {\n    const dirName = path.basename(skillDir).normalize('NFKC');\n    if (dirName !== name) {\n      errors.push(`Directory name '${dirName}' must match skill name '${name}'`);\n    }\n  }\n\n  return errors;\n}\n\n/**\n * Validate the description field.\n */\nfunction validateDescription(metadata) {\n  if (metadata.description === undefined || metadata.description === null) {\n    return ['Missing required field in frontmatter: description'];\n  }\n\n  if (typeof metadata.description !== 'string' || metadata.description.trim() === '') {\n    return [\"Field 'description' must be a non-empty string\"];\n  }\n\n  if (metadata.description.length > MAX_DESCRIPTION_LENGTH) {\n    return [`Description exceeds 1024 character limit (${metadata.description.length} chars)`];\n  }\n\n  return [];\n}\n\n/**\n * Validate the optional compatibility field.\n */\nfunction validateCompatibility(metadata) {\n  if (metadata.compatibility === undefined || metadata.compatibility === null) {\n    return [];\n  }\n\n  if (typeof metadata.compatibility !== 'string') {\n    return [\"Field 'compatibility' must be a string\"];\n  }\n\n  if (metadata.compatibility.length > MAX_COMPATIBILITY_LENGTH) {\n    return [`Compatibility exceeds 500 character limit (${metadata.compatibility.length} chars)`];\n  }\n\n  return [];\n}\n\nmodule.exports = {\n  validate,\n  validateMetadata,\n  MAX_SKILL_NAME_LENGTH,\n  MAX_DESCRIPTION_LENGTH,\n  MAX_COMPATIBILITY_LENGTH,\n  ALLOWED_FIELDS,\n};\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/tools/skill-check/lib/prompt.js",
  "content": "'use strict';\n\nconst path = require('node:path');\nconst { readProperties } = require('./properties');\nconst { findSkillMd } = require('./parser');\n\n/**\n * Escape XML special characters.\n * @param {string} text\n * @returns {string}\n */\nfunction escapeXml(text) {\n  return text.replace(/&/g, '&amp;').replace(/</g, '&lt;').replace(/>/g, '&gt;');\n}\n\n/**\n * Generate an <available_skills> XML block for agent system prompts.\n * @param {string[]} skillDirs - Paths to skill directories\n * @returns {string}\n */\nfunction toPrompt(skillDirs) {\n  const lines = ['<available_skills>'];\n\n  for (const dir of skillDirs) {\n    const resolved = path.resolve(dir);\n    const props = readProperties(resolved);\n    const skillMdPath = findSkillMd(resolved);\n\n    lines.push('<skill>');\n    lines.push(`<name>${escapeXml(props.name)}</name>`);\n    lines.push(`<description>${escapeXml(props.description)}</description>`);\n    lines.push(`<location>${skillMdPath}</location>`);\n    lines.push('</skill>');\n  }\n\n  lines.push('</available_skills>');\n  return lines.join('\\n');\n}\n\nmodule.exports = { toPrompt, escapeXml };\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/tools/skill-check/skill-check.js",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env node\n'use strict';\n\nconst { Command } = require('commander');\nconst { resolve } = require('node:path');\nconst { resolveSkillPath } = require('./lib/parser');\nconst { validate } = require('./lib/validator');\nconst { readProperties } = require('./lib/properties');\nconst { toPrompt } = require('./lib/prompt');\n\nconst program = new Command();\n\nprogram\n  .name('skill-check')\n  .description('Validate agent skills against the agentskills.io specification')\n  .version('1.0.0');\n\nprogram\n  .command('validate')\n  .description('Validate a skill directory')\n  .argument('<path>', 'Path to skill directory or SKILL.md file')\n  .action((inputPath) => {\n    const skillDir = resolveSkillPath(inputPath);\n    const errors = validate(skillDir);\n\n    if (errors.length > 0) {\n      for (const error of errors) {\n        process.stderr.write(`  - ${error}\\n`);\n      }\n      process.exit(1);\n    }\n\n    console.log(`Valid skill: ${resolve(skillDir)}`);\n  });\n\nprogram\n  .command('read-properties')\n  .description('Read skill frontmatter as JSON')\n  .argument('<path>', 'Path to skill directory or SKILL.md file')\n  .action((inputPath) => {\n    const skillDir = resolveSkillPath(inputPath);\n    try {\n      const props = readProperties(skillDir);\n      console.log(JSON.stringify(props.toDict(), null, 2));\n    } catch (error) {\n      process.stderr.write(`${error.message}\\n`);\n      process.exit(1);\n    }\n  });\n\nprogram\n  .command('to-prompt')\n  .description('Generate <available_skills> XML block for agent system prompts')\n  .argument('<paths...>', 'One or more paths to skill directories or SKILL.md files')\n  .action((paths) => {\n    try {\n      const resolved = paths.map((p) => resolveSkillPath(p));\n      const output = toPrompt(resolved);\n      console.log(output);\n    } catch (error) {\n      process.stderr.write(`${error.message}\\n`);\n      process.exit(1);\n    }\n  });\n\nprogram.parse();\n"
}

     6→
     7→# Step 6: Validate
     8→
     9→## STEP GOAL:
    10→
    11→To validate the compiled SKILL.md content against the agentskills.io specification using skills_ref, auto-fix any validation failures, and confirm spec compliance before artifact generation.
    12→
    13→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    14→
    15→### Universal Rules:
    16→
    17→- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    18→- 🎯 ALWAYS follow the exact instructions in the step file
    19→- ⚙️ TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK: If any instruction references a tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context thread
    20→- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
    21→
    22→### Role Reinforcement:
    23→
    24→- ✅ You are a skill compilation engine performing quality assurance
    25→- ✅ Validation ensures spec compliance — it does not modify content semantics
    26→- ✅ Tool unavailability means skip validation, not halt workflow
    27→
    28→### Step-Specific Rules:
    29→
    30→- 🎯 Focus ONLY on validating compiled content against spec
    31→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to add new content — only fix spec compliance issues
    32→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to write files — content stays in context until step-07
    33→- 💬 If auto-fix fails, report issues clearly but proceed (warn, don't halt)
    34→- ⚙️ If skills_ref unavailable: skip validation, add warning to evidence report
    35→
    36→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    37→
    38→- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
    39→- 💾 Validation results are added to evidence-report content in context
    40→- 📖 Auto-fix pattern: validate → fix → re-validate (once)
    41→- 🚫 Maximum one auto-fix attempt per validation failure
    42→
    43→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    44→
    45→- Available: All compiled content from step-05 (SKILL.md, metadata.json, etc.)
    46→- Focus: Spec compliance validation and auto-fix
    47→- Limits: Do NOT add new content or modify extraction data
    48→- Dependencies: Compiled content must exist from step-05
    49→
    50→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    51→
    52→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    53→
    54→### 1. Check Tool Availability
    55→
    56→**If skills_ref tool is available:**
    57→Continue to validation steps.
    58→
    59→**If skills_ref tool is NOT available:**
    60→- Perform manual frontmatter compliance check (see step 3 fallback below)
    61→- Add note to evidence-report content: "Spec validation performed manually — skills_ref tool unavailable"
    62→
    63→### 2. Validate Schema
    64→
    65→Use `skills_ref.validate_schema()` against the compiled SKILL.md content.
    66→
    67→**Check:**
    68→- Required sections present (Overview, Quick Start, API Reference, Type Definitions)
    69→- Section order follows agentskills.io standard
    70→- Frontmatter contains required fields (name, description) with no disallowed fields
    71→- Provenance citations present in API Reference entries
    72→
    73→**If validation passes:** Record "Schema: PASS" in evidence-report content.
    74→
    75→**If validation fails:**
    76→1. Identify specific failures
    77→2. Attempt auto-fix (structural adjustments only — never invent content)
    78→3. Re-validate once
    79→4. If still failing: record "Schema: FAIL — {specific issues}" in evidence-report, add warnings, but proceed
    80→
    81→### 3. Validate Frontmatter
    82→
    83→**If skills_ref available:** Use `skills_ref.validate_frontmatter()` against the SKILL.md frontmatter.
    84→
    85→**If skills_ref NOT available (fallback):** Perform manual frontmatter compliance check.
    86→
    87→**Check (agentskills.io specification):**
    88→
    89→- [ ] Frontmatter present — file starts with `---` and has closing `---`
    90→- [ ] `name` field — present, non-empty, lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens only, 1-64 chars
    91→- [ ] `name` matches skill output directory name
    92→- [ ] `description` field — present, non-empty, 1-1024 characters
    93→- [ ] No unknown fields — only `name`, `description`, `license`, `compatibility`, `metadata`, `allowed-tools` are permitted
    94→- [ ] `version` and `author` are NOT in frontmatter (they belong in metadata.json)
    95→
    96→**If validation passes:** Record "Frontmatter: PASS" in evidence-report content.
    97→
    98→**If validation fails:**
    99→1. Auto-fix frontmatter (these are deterministic fixes — remove disallowed fields, add missing required fields)
   100→2. Re-validate once
   101→3. Record result in evidence-report
   102→
   103→### 4. Validate metadata.json
   104→
   105→Cross-check metadata.json content against extraction inventory:
   106→- `stats.exports_documented` matches actual documented exports
   107→- `stats.exports_total` matches total extracted exports
   108→- `stats.coverage` is accurate (documented / total)
   109→- `confidence_t1`, `confidence_t2`, `confidence_t3` match actual counts
   110→- `spec_version` is "1.3"
   111→
   112→Auto-fix any discrepancies (these are computed values).
   113→
   114→### 5. Update Evidence Report
   115→
   116→Add validation results to the evidence-report content in context:
   117→
   118→```markdown
   119→## Validation Results
   120→- Schema: {pass/fail}
   121→- Frontmatter: {pass/fail}
   122→- Metadata: {pass/fail}
   123→
   124→## Warnings
   125→- {any warnings from validation}
   126→```
   127→
   128→### 6. Menu Handling Logic
   129→
   130→**Auto-proceed step — no user interaction.**
   131→
   132→After validation is complete (or skipped if tools unavailable), immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
   133→
   134→#### EXECUTION RULES:
   135→
   136→- This is an auto-proceed validation step with no user choices
   137→- Tool unavailability is a skip, not a halt
   138→- Validation failures are warnings — proceed to artifact generation
   139→- Proceed directly to next step after validation completes
   140→
   141→## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
   142→
   143→ONLY WHEN validation is complete (or skipped) and evidence-report content is updated will you proceed to load `{nextStepFile}` for artifact generation.
   144→
   145→---
   146→
   147→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   148→
   149→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   150→
   151→- Schema validation attempted (or skipped with warning if tool unavailable)
   152→- Frontmatter validation attempted
   153→- Metadata cross-check performed
   154→- Auto-fix applied for deterministic failures
   155→- Evidence report updated with validation results
   156→- Warnings recorded for any failures
   157→- Auto-proceeded to step-07
   158→
   159→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   160→

   162→- Halting on skills_ref unavailability (should skip with warning)
   163→- Adding new content during validation (only structural fixes allowed)
   164→- Not recording validation results in evidence report
   165→- Attempting more than one auto-fix cycle per failure
   166→
   167→**Master Rule:** Validation informs, it does not block. Record results, fix what's deterministic, warn about the rest, and proceed.
